{"parentUuid":"e6b21206-5484-47a7-a503-37d84b5c2db8","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"056dda7b-ca9a-42cf-8c23-f13581158977","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J7i6D3qZc74r4TpGgZEZJZ","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Turn notes/usage-ledger.csv into a plan-vs-actual budget report.\n\nUsage:\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py                 # summary + forecast\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 4   # 4 tickets still to do\n\nWhy positive deltas: the 5-hour and weekly meters RESET (drop to 0) when their\nwindow rolls over, and the weekly one only ever climbs inside a week. Summing\nonly the increases between consecutive rows measures what you actually spent\nand ignores the resets. That is what spend() does, and it is your exercise.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nLEDGER = Path(\"notes/usage-ledger.csv\")\n\n\ndef spend(series: list[float | None]) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Total rise across a meter that can reset to a lower value.\n\n    Sum every increase between consecutive readings; ignore blanks (None) and\n    any drop, which means the window reset.\n\n    >>> spend([10.0, 12.0, 15.0])\n    5.0\n    >>> spend([80.0, 5.0, 9.0])\n    4.0\n    >>> spend([None, 4.0, None, 7.0])\n    3.0\n    \"\"\"\n    readings = [v for v in series if v is not None]\n    total = 0.0\n    for prev, cur in zip(readings, readings[1:]):\n        if cur > prev:\n            total += cur - prev\n    return total\n\ndef read_rows(path: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    with path.open(newline=\"\") as f:\n        return list(csv.DictReader(f))\n\n\ndef to_float(text: str | None) -> float | None:\n    try:\n        return float(text) if text not in (None, \"\") else None\n    except ValueError:\n        return None\n\n\ndef by_phase(rows: list[dict]) -> dict[str, dict]:\n    phases: dict[str, dict] = defaultdict(\n        lambda: {\"turns\": 0, \"five\": [], \"weekly\": [], \"models\": set(), \"providers\": set()}\n    )\n    for row in rows:\n        phase = phases[row.get(\"phase\") or \"unlabelled\"]\n        phase[\"turns\"] += 1\n        phase[\"five\"].append(to_float(row.get(\"five_h_pct\")))\n        phase[\"weekly\"].append(to_float(row.get(\"weekly_pct\")))\n        if row.get(\"model\"):\n            phase[\"models\"].add(row[\"model\"])\n        if row.get(\"provider\"):\n            phase[\"providers\"].add(row[\"provider\"])\n    return phases\n\n\ndef ticket_phases(phases: dict[str, dict]) -> list[str]:\n    return sorted(p for p in phases if p.upper().startswith(\"T\") and p[1:].isdigit())\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--ledger\", type=Path, default=LEDGER)\n    ap.add_argument(\"--remaining\", type=int, default=0, help=\"tickets not yet built\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    if not args.ledger.exists():\n        raise SystemExit(f\"No ledger at {args.ledger}. Is the Stop hook registered?\")\n    rows = read_rows(args.ledger)\n    if not rows:\n        raise SystemExit(\"Ledger is empty.\")\n    phases = by_phase(rows)\n\n    print(f\"{'phase':<14}{'turns':>6}{'5h spent':>10}{'weekly':>9}  models / provider\")\n    for name, data in sorted(phases.items()):\n        mix = \",\".join(sorted(data[\"models\"])) or \"?\"\n        prov = \",\".join(sorted(data[\"providers\"] - {\"anthropic\"}))\n        print(f\"{name:<14}{data['turns']:>6}{spend(data['five']):>9.1f}%\"\n              f\"{spend(data['weekly']):>8.1f}%  {mix}{(' @' + prov) if prov else ''}\")\n\n    tickets = ticket_phases(phases)\n    if tickets:\n        per = sum(spend(phases[t][\"weekly\"]) for t in tickets) / len(tickets)\n        print(f\"\\nTickets done: {len(tickets)} · average weekly cost per ticket: {per:.1f}%\")\n        last = next((to_float(r.get(\"weekly_pct\")) for r in reversed(rows)\n                     if to_float(r.get(\"weekly_pct\")) is not None), None)\n        if last is not None and args.remaining:\n            need, headroom = per * args.remaining, 100 - last\n            verdict = \"FITS\" if need <= headroom * 0.8 else \"TIGHT\" if need <= headroom else \"OVER BUDGET\"\n            print(f\"Forecast: {args.remaining} tickets need ~{need:.0f}% of the weekly cap; \"\n                  f\"{headroom:.0f}% remains → {verdict}\")\n            if verdict != \"FITS\":\n                print(\"Fix in this order: 1) lower effort  2) switch to Sonnet/Haiku  \"\n                      \"3) /clear more often  4) split fat tickets  5) Plan B backend (declare it)\")\n    else:\n        print(\"\\nNo ticket phases yet. Write the ticket id into .claude/state/phase \"\n              \"at the start of each /implement session.\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n------\nphase          turns  5h spent   weekly  models / provider\nT01                9      5.0%     0.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nT02               29     11.0%     1.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nT03               37      9.0%     1.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nT04               15     11.0%     2.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nT05               11      4.0%     1.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nT06               10      6.0%     1.0%  Sonnet 5.5\nfinal             24     26.0%     7.0%  Sonnet 5.5\ngrill              6      3.0%     0.0%  Opus 5.5\nspec               3      3.0%     1.0%  Opus 5.5\ntickets            7      5.0%     1.0%  Opus 5.5\nunlabelled        26     18.0%     4.0%  Opus 5.5,Sonnet 5\n\nTickets done: 6 · average weekly cost per ticket: 1.0%\n------\n11:Another decision that was mine was on the actual content of a typical AWM \"post\". I think AWM is more boring than Claude anticipates because we only have one type of meeting, and it is practically always the same day and time, without fail. So I explained that \"the most common 99% of the time will be the weekly Monday study hall reminder, but other things can be posted, such as a cancelled meeting, snack update etc.\" This was helpful because after this input, Claude had a more clear understanding of the organization. \r\n37:\tHonestly when talking about budgeted tokens I greatly overestimated this project. I was about 3-5x under my proposed budget; I estimated each ticket would use 25-30% of a 5h window per ticket but on average it used about 8%. The setup and pre-exercises accounted for the largest chunk of tokens, this is the “unlabelled” section that took up about 18% of my 5h window. The planning section of this project was projected to use 35% of my 5h window and it actually used 11%. Overall, this mini-project only used about 14% of my weekly cap when I thought it would use 30-45%. I am honestly surprised at this efficiency because I opted to use high thinking during the planning stages and I feared that would waste a lot of my tokens. I also originally planned to use Haiku for efficiency purposes but ended up not switching to it. An observation from the tickets is that T02 and T04 cost the most, both of them used about 11% of my 5h window. However, T03 had the most turns. This just shows that cost is not equivalent to chats sent.\r\n39:Since I overestimated by so much I never had to use the plan I put in place if I was at risk of going over budget. Also, note about the budget plan: I accidentally wrote “ 225-2705” when I meant “225–270%.” I am glad I overestimated because I would much rather that than the alternative. This thinking is why I believe the overestimation happened in the first place. This was my first time doing anything like this, next time, I would base my budget off this project.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"f92d75ab-be17-4b7a-9d32-bb9f643314d8","timestamp":"2026-10-05T18:20:34.619Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Turn notes/usage-ledger.csv into a plan-vs-actual budget report.\n\nUsage:\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py                 # summary + forecast\n    uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 4   # 4 tickets still to do\n\nWhy positive deltas: the 5-hour and weekly meters RESET (drop to 0) when their\nwindow rolls over, and the weekly one only ever climbs inside a week. {"parentUuid":"bdadd74b-968e-42fa-9917-8f38287e9d69","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjZ3ud9M93omgrVceXgf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"**Most of the report's token percentages match the ledger, but the weekly-cap figure doesn't.** I read the report, `scripts/usage_report.py` and its output, and `notes/usage-ledger.csv`. I changed nothing.\n\n**Claims that check out** (figures are the 5-hour window, from `usage_report.py`):\n\n| Report says | Ledger / script | Match |\n|---|---|---|\n| Each ticket averaged \"about 8%\" of a 5h window | T01–T06 spent 5, 11, 9, 11, 4 and 6, which averages 7.7% | ✓ |\n| T02 and T04 cost the most, \"about 11%\" each | T02 = 11%, T04 = 11% | ✓ |\n| T03 had the most turns | T03 has 37 turns, T02 has 29 | ✓ |\n| \"unlabelled\" took \"about 18%\" | 18.0% | ✓ |\n| Planning used 11% | grill 3 + spec 3 + tickets 5 = 11% | ✓ |\n\n**Claim that's off:** the report says the project \"only used about 14% of my weekly cap.\" The ledger gives a higher number, depending on how you count:\n- **Per-phase weekly spend** adds up to 19%: 0+1+1+2+1+1+7+0+1+1+4. The `final` phase alone is 7%.\n- **Whole-ledger weekly spend** is 22%, using the same `spend()` function over the full series. The last reading, on 2026-10-05, is 23%.\n- **14% is not a total I can reproduce.** The nearest I get is 12%, which is everything except `final`. Check whether the 14% was written before the `final` phase was logged.\n\nBecause `final` is excluded, the \"3-5x under budget\" framing for the weekly cap is too generous. 22% against your 30–45% estimate is roughly 1.4–2x under, not 3–5x. The \"3-5x\" claim does hold for per-ticket 5h cost, where 8% was measured against a 25–30% estimate.\n\n**Smaller notes:**\n- `final` used 26% of the 5h window, more than any ticket. The report doesn't mention it.\n- The \"about 11%\" planning figure and \"about 18%\" setup figure leave out `final`, so those are fine as written.\n- The ledger has two uncommitted new rows. I used the working-tree version, so the committed ledger may differ slightly.\n- I didn't check the \"225–270%\" and \"35%\" budget-plan figures, because the original plan isn't in the files I read.\n\nIf you want, I can suggest corrected wording for the weekly-cap sentence."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1048,"cache_read_input_tokens":49819,"output_tokens":859,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1048,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":859,"cache_read_input_tokens":49819,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1048,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1048},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"3567de12-2120-4f89-9e60-171f7d45eeb5","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfjZ3uJ44fxxWZ4BtEN9N","type":"assistant","uuid":"e6fb4921-e86c-4c05-91bc-93b0aef1f0b7","timestamp":"2026-10-05T18:20:50.626Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","version":"2.1.289","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"dbeea0a2-086c-44ff-8308-b7b5d622e0ed","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"e0fecbc5-d811-4cb2-912b-336ba1044eb8","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013a6ikfgwgfAiBTSm1yz14U","type":"tool_result","content":"--- line 181\nI've read your field notes, brief, the manual's seven capabilities, both ADRs, the rubric, and the template code; `CONTEXT.md` is still empty, so nothing conflicts with a glossary yet. Here is round 1.\n\nThis version of the skill asks in rounds rather than one question at a time. Answer by number, in any order, and override my recommendation wherever AWM's reality says otherwise.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q1 - One account per person, or one per position?** Your brief says \"passwords are passed down from spring semester to fall semester each year.\" That can mean two different things:\n\n- **(a) Per position:** there is one \"Treasurer\" login, and the password is handed to whoever holds the job next.\n- **(b) Per person:** every officer has their own login; each year the co-VPs create accounts for new officers and deactivate the outgoing ones.\n\nScenario: last year's Social Chair graduates in May. In September, can they still log in?\n\n➡️ **(b), per person.** Under (a) the old Social Chair still knows the password, and capability 6 (\"a deactivated user cannot log in\") has nothing to deactivate. The only thing passed down would be the co-VP handover: outgoing co-VPs create the incoming co-VPs' accounts before they leave.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2 - What is the weekly thing called, and is it the only kind?** The manual calls it a \"post\", and your brief says \"post the Monday Study Hall info\" and \"a link to the website post.\" Two things to settle:\n\n1. Is **Post** the word officers would use, or would they say \"announcement\", \"update\", or something else?\n2. Is the weekly Study Hall post the *only* kind, or do other things get posted (a cancelled Study Hall, a finals-week change, a social event from the Social Chair)?\n\n➡️ **One content type, called Post**, meaning any dated news item. The weekly Study Hall post is the most common one, not a special type. Avoid \"announcement\" and \"update\" so we don't end up with two designs.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q3 - Where does the standing time and place live?** Your brief's pain point is \"the time and place is in 3 files.\" Hayes 311, Mondays 8–10pm has to live in exactly one place:\n\n- **(a)** A **Study Hall page** holds it; weekly posts only say what is different this week.\n- **(b)** Site-wide settings fields (day, time, room) shown in the header of every page.\n- **(c)** Retyped in every weekly post.\n\nScenario: Study Hall moves to another room for one week only, versus moving permanently.\n\n➡️ **(a).** The page is the single source and a permanent move is one edit. A one-week change is a post, and the page stays untouched. (b) is a new feature outside the seven capabilities, with Stage 2 due Tuesday Oct 6. (c) recreates the problem you have now.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q4 - Whose computer is the CMS on, and what does \"publish\" mean?** The assignment fixes that the admin console runs on a laptop, never on the internet (ADR-001). The database is not in git, so content does not travel between machines. So \"publish\" is really two steps, close to your field note 2:\n\n1. An officer marks a post **Published** in the console. It is still only on that laptop.\n2. Someone runs `cms publish` and `cms deploy` in a terminal. Now members can see it.\n\nWhere does that one copy live, and who does step 2? And what would officers call each step?\n\n➡️ **One copy on a co-VP's laptop**, with the co-VPs responsible for step 2. Use two words: **Published** for the status an officer sets, and **Go live** for pushing the site to the public. This clashes with \"a new officer can post without learning HTML\" if step 2 needs a terminal, so I will ask next round whether the console needs a button for it.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q5 - Which pages exist, and who creates them?** The brief says the site tells people when and where Study Hall is, how to join, and links to the snack request form. It also says editors edit pages but never delete them. It doesn't say who *creates* them.\n\n➡️ **Three pages: Study Hall, Join, Snacks** (the last one is mostly the Google Form link). **Only co-VPs create and delete pages**; editors edit the text of existing ones. Pages are the site's permanent structure, and a new Treasurer adding a fourth page to the navigation by accident is the kind of thing your \"never delete\" rule is trying to prevent.\n\n---\n\nParked for later rounds because they depend on these answers: what delete means for posts, whether an editor can unpublish a page, slugs for weekly posts with near-identical titles, what the home page shows, whose name appears on a post, the yearly handover, and the out-of-scope list.\n--- line 204\nRound 1 is settled and `CONTEXT.md` now has 15 terms from your answers. Two of them are words I chose, so veto them if they're wrong:\n\n- **Account** for a position's login (instead of \"user\"), since you decided the login belongs to the position, not a person.\n- **Study Hall reminder** for the weekly Monday post, taken from your Q2 answer.\n\nOne thing to reconcile: in Q3 you picked a Study Hall page, but your three pages in Q5 don't include one. I read that as the Home page being the single place the time and room live. Q9 confirms it.\n\nHere is round 2.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q6 - How many accounts are there?** You have two Co-VPs and two Co-Presidents. Is that:\n\n- **(a) Four accounts**, one per position: Co-VP, Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer. Both Co-VPs use the same login.\n- **(b) Six accounts**: each Co-VP and each Co-President gets their own.\n\nScenario: there is only one Admin account and someone deactivates it or switches it to Editor. Nobody can manage accounts any more.\n\n➡️ **(a), four accounts**, which fits your \"it isn't that top secret\" answer. Add one rule: the last active Admin account can never be deactivated or changed to Editor.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q7 - What happens to a password at Handover, and when one is forgotten?** Scenario: the new Treasurer forgets the password in October. Who fixes it, and how?\n\n➡️ **Co-VPs can set a new password for any account from the Accounts screen.** At Handover the old password can simply be passed on, or a Co-VP can change it. No \"forgot my password\" email, because the console is on one laptop and cannot send mail.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q8 - Whose name is on a post?** Capability 3 requires an author. With per-position accounts the author is a position, not a person. Your example email is signed \"- AWM <3\".\n\n➡️ **The console shows the position** (\"Social Chair\") in the content list, so Co-VPs can see who posted what. **The public site shows only the date**, with no author line, so posts read as coming from AWM like the email does.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q9 - What exactly is the Home page?** I understand it as: the standing Study Hall info (Mondays 8–10pm, Hayes 311) as editable text, with the latest post shown underneath. Three things to decide:\n\n1. Can anyone delete or unpublish Home?\n2. Does it show only the latest post, or a few?\n3. Where do older posts go, and does each post get its own link for the weekly email?\n\n➡️ **Home always exists**: nobody, including a Co-VP, can delete it or set it back to Draft, and Editors can edit its text. **It shows the single latest published post in full.** **Each post has its own link** for the email, and older posts sit on an automatic \"Past posts\" list linked from Home. That list is not a Page and nobody creates it.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q10 - What does deleting a post mean?** Your brief says Editors publish and delete posts. Scenario: Monday at 7pm the Treasurer deletes the Social Chair's Study Hall reminder by mistake.\n\n- May an Editor delete any post, or only ones from their own account?\n- Is it gone for good, or recoverable?\n\n➡️ **Any Editor can delete any post, after a \"Are you sure?\" confirmation, and it is permanent.** A trash bin is extra work with the deadline on Tuesday, and a reminder is a few lines to retype. The post leaves the public site at the next Go live.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q11 - Can an Editor change a page's status?** Setting a Published page back to Draft removes it from the navigation, which for Visitors is the same as deleting it. Your brief says an Editor must never delete a page.\n\n➡️ **No. Only Co-VPs change a page's status.** Editors change the text only. An Editor's text change to a Published page reaches Visitors at the next Go live, with no approval step.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q12 - Is Go live a button or a terminal command?** Co-VPs change every year and your brief says each new person has a different level of computing expertise. Right now Go live means typing two commands in a terminal.\n\n➡️ **A \"Go live\" button on the dashboard, Co-VP only.** It runs the same two steps as the commands. Editors don't get it, since you said Co-VPs are responsible for step 2 and it is their laptop.\n\n---\n\nOne offer: your Q1 decision (accounts belong to positions, passwords passed down) meets the bar for an ADR. It is hard to reverse, surprising to a later reader, and you chose it over per-person accounts for a stated reason. Say yes and I'll write `ADR-003` with your reasoning.\n\nStill parked: slugs for near-identical weekly titles, what the dashboard counts, and the out-of-scope list.\n--- line 232\nRound 2 is settled. `CONTEXT.md` now also defines **Author**, **Weekly email** (your note that it matches the post plus a link), **Home**, and **Past posts**, and the Account entry says the two Co-VPs share one account.\n\nHere is round 3, which I expect to be the last full round.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q13 - What does an account sign in with?** The template's test helper signs in with an email address and a password, and the seeded demo accounts are `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. I can't see the grader's walkthrough script, but it most likely does the same. Does AWM have an email address per position, one club address, or neither?\n\n➡️ **Keep an email-shaped sign-in**, one address per account (for example a `treasurer@...` style address, real or not). The console never sends mail, so the address is only a login name. Each account also has a display name that is the position (\"Treasurer\"), and that is what shows as Author.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q14 - What is the link to a weekly post?** The link (the slug) is built from the title, and it is what goes in the Weekly email. Nearly every post is titled something like \"Study Hall tonight!\", so titles will collide. A new officer will also never have heard of a slug.\n\n➡️ **The link is made automatically from the date plus the title** (for example `2026-10-05-study-hall-tonight`), and officers never type it. **It stops changing once the post is Published**, so a link already sent in an email never breaks, even if someone fixes a typo in the title. The Monday order becomes: write the post, mark it Published, a Co-VP presses Go live, then send the email with the link.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q15 - How does a Visitor know which Monday a post means?** Your example says \"We are CURRENTLY having our study hall tonight.\" Scenario: a curious student opens Home on Thursday and reads \"tonight\".\n\n➡️ **Every post shows its date with the weekday** (\"Monday, October 5\") right above the text, on Home and on its own page. That date is when the post was first Published. Nothing expires or hides automatically.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q16 - What does the dashboard tell an officer?** Capability 5 needs counts and a list you can filter. Scenario: the Social Chair marks the reminder Published at 6pm and leaves. Nobody presses Go live, so the email link leads nowhere.\n\n➡️ **Counts of Draft posts, Published posts, and Pages**, with the number of Accounts shown to Co-VPs only. Add **\"Last Go live: date and time\"** and a plain warning when something has changed since then (\"2 changes are not live yet\"). The content list filters by Draft/Published and by Post/Page, newest first.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q17 - What is deactivating an account for?** With one account per position, nobody \"leaves\". Two scenarios: AWM has no Social Chair for a year, or AWM adds a new position such as an outreach chair.\n\n➡️ **Co-VPs deactivate an account when its position is vacant and create one when a position is added**, choosing its Role. A deactivated account cannot sign in, but **its posts stay on the site** and can be reactivated later. Accounts are never deleted.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q18 - What is explicitly out of scope?** My proposed list of things this CMS will not do:\n\n- Send the Weekly email (it stays manual, per your brief)\n- Host the snack request form (Join & Snacks only links to the Google Form)\n- Scheduled posts or a calendar\n- Comments, or any Visitor login\n- Image uploads (this means no uploaded officer photos on About)\n- Categories, tags, or search\n- A trash bin or undo for deleted posts\n- \"Forgot my password\" emails\n- A \"copy last week's reminder\" button\n\n➡️ **All nine are out.** The last one would be useful for AWM, since 99% of posts are the same reminder, so I'd name it as the first future improvement in your report rather than build it before Tuesday. Tell me if officers would expect any of these, especially photos on About.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q19 - Record the shared-accounts decision as an ADR?** This is the offer from last round.\n\n➡️ **Yes.** `ADR-003` would say accounts belong to positions and passwords are passed down at Handover, chosen over per-person accounts because, in your words, it isn't that top secret and former officers will hardly think about it.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"61126b2d-eb4f-478f-a37e-7861bd7ab895","timestamp":"2026-10-05T18:31:48.771Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"--- line 181\nI've read your field notes, brief, the manual's seven capabilities, both ADRs, the rubric, and the template code; `CONTEXT.md` is still empty, so nothing conflicts with a glossary yet. Here is round 1.\n\nThis version of the skill asks in rounds rather than one question at a time. Answer by number, in any order, and override my recommendation wherever AWM's reality says otherwise.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q1 - One account per person, or one per position?** Your brief says \"passwords are passed down from spring semester to fall semester each year.\" That can mean two different things:\n\n- **(a) Per position:** there is one \"Treasurer\" login, and the password is handed to whoever holds the job next.\n- **(b) Per person:** every officer has their own login; each year the co-VPs create accounts for new officers and deactivate the outgoing ones.\n\nScenario: last year's Social Chair graduates in May. In September, can they still log in?\n\n➡️ **(b), per person.** Under (a) the old Social Chair still knows the password, and capability 6 (\"a deactivated user cannot log in\") has nothing to deactivate. The only thing passed down would be the co-VP handover: outgoing co-VPs create the incoming co-VPs' accounts before they leave.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2 - What is the weekly thing called, and is it the only kind?** The manual calls it a \"post\", and your brief says \"post the Monday Study Hall info\" and \"a link to the website post.\" Two things to settle:\n\n1. Is **Post** the word officers would use, or would they say \"announcement\", \"update\", or something else?\n2. Is the weekly Study Hall post the *only* kind, or do other things get posted (a cancelled Study Hall, a finals-week change, a social event from the Social Chair)?\n\n➡️ **One content type, called Post**, meaning any dated news item. The weekly Study Hall post is the most common one, not a special type. Avoid \"announcement\" and \"update\" so we don't end up with two designs.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q3 - Where does the standing time and place live?** Your brief's pain point is \"the time and place is in 3 files.\" Hayes 311, Mondays 8–10pm has to live in exactly one place:\n\n- **(a)** A **Study Hall page** holds it; weekly posts only say what is different this week.\n- **(b)** Site-wide settings fields (day, time, room) shown in the header of every page.\n- **(c)** Retyped in every weekly post.\n\nScenario: Study Hall moves to another room for one week only, versus moving permanently.\n\n➡️ **(a).** The page is the single source and a permanent move is one edit. A one-week change is a post, and the page stays untouched. (b) is a new feature outside the seven capabilities, with Stage 2 due Tuesday Oct 6. (c) recreates the problem you have now.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q4 - Whose computer is the CMS on, and what does \"publish\" mean?** The assignment fixes that the admin console runs on a laptop, never on the internet (ADR-001). The database is not in git, so content does not travel between machines. So \"publish\" is really two steps, close to your field note 2:\n\n1. An officer marks a post **Published** in the console. It is still only on that laptop.\n2. Someone runs `cms publish` and `cms deploy` in a terminal. Now members can see it.\n\nWhere does that one copy live, and who does step 2? And what would officers call each step?\n\n➡️ **One copy on a co-VP's laptop**, with the co-VPs responsible for step 2. Use two words: **Published** for the status an officer sets, and **Go live** for pushing the site to the public. This clashes with \"a new officer can post without learning HTML\" if step 2 needs a terminal, so I will ask next round whether the console needs a button for it.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q5 - Which pages exist, and who creates them?** The brief says the site tells people when and where Study Hall is, how to join, and links to the snack request form. It also says editors edit pages but never delete them. It doesn't say who *creates* them.\n\n➡️ **Three pages: Study Hall, Join, Snacks** (the last one is mostly the Google Form link). **Only co-VPs create and delete pages**; editors edit the text of existing ones. Pages are the site's permanent structure, and a new Treasurer adding a fourth page to the navigation by accident is the kind of thing your \"never delete\" rule is trying to prevent.\n\n---\n\nParked for later rounds because they depend on these answers: what delete means for posts, whether an editor can unpublish a page, slugs for weekly posts with near-identical titles, what the home page shows, whose name appears on a post, the yearly handover, and the out-of-scope list.\n--- line 204\nRound 1 is settled and `CONTEXT.md` now has 15 terms from your answers. Two of them are words I chose, so veto them if they're wrong:\n\n- **Account** for a position's login (instead of \"user\"), since you decided the login belongs to the position, not a person.\n- **Study Hall reminder** for the weekly Monday post, taken from your Q2 answer.\n\nOne thing to reconcile: in Q3 you picked a Study Hall page, but your three pages in Q5 don't include one. I read that as the Home page being the single place the time and room live. Q9 confirms it.\n\nHere is round 2.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q6 - How many accounts are there?** You have two Co-VPs and two Co-Presidents. Is that:\n\n- **(a) Four accounts**, one per position: Co-VP, Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer. Both Co-VPs use the same login.\n- **(b) Six accounts**: each Co-VP and each Co-President gets their own.\n\nScenario: there is only one Admin account and someone deactivates it or switches it to Editor. Nobody can manage accounts any more.\n\n➡️ **(a), four accounts**, which fits your \"it isn't that top secret\" answer. Add one rule: the last active Admin account can never be deactivated or changed to Editor.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q7 - What happens to a password at Handover, and when one is forgotten?** Scenario: the new Treasurer forgets the password in October. Who fixes it, and how?\n\n➡️ **Co-VPs can set a new password for any account from the Accounts screen.** At Handover the old password can simply be passed on, or a Co-VP can change it. No \"forgot my password\" email, because the console is on one laptop and cannot send mail.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q8 - Whose name is on a post?** Capability 3 requires an author. With per-position accounts the author is a position, not a person. Your example email is signed \"- AWM <3\".\n\n➡️ **The console shows the position** (\"Social Chair\") in the content list, so Co-VPs can see who posted what. **The public site shows only the date**, with no author line, so posts read as coming from AWM like the email does.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q9 - What exactly is the Home page?** I understand it as: the standing Study Hall info (Mondays 8–10pm, Hayes 311) as editable text, with the latest post shown underneath. Three things to decide:\n\n1. Can anyone delete or unpublish Home?\n2. Does it show only the latest post, or a few?\n3. Where do older posts go, and does each post get its own link for the weekly email?\n\n➡️ **Home always exists**: nobody, including a Co-VP, can delete it or set it back to Draft, and Editors can edit its text. **It shows the single latest published post in full.** **Each post has its own link** for the email, and older posts sit on an automatic \"Past posts\" list linked from Home. That list is not a Page and nobody creates it.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q10 - What does deleting a post mean?** Your brief says Editors publish and delete posts. Scenario: Monday at 7pm the Treasurer deletes the Social Chair's Study Hall reminder by mistake.\n\n- May an Editor delete any post, or only ones from their own account?\n- Is it gone for good, or recoverable?\n\n➡️ **Any Editor can delete any post, after a \"Are you sure?\" confirmation, and it is permanent.** A trash bin is extra work with the deadline on Tuesday, and a reminder is a few lines to retype. The post leaves the public site at the next Go live.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q11 - Can an Editor change a page's status?** Setting a Published page back to Draft removes it from the navigation, which for Visitors is the same as deleting it. Your brief says an Editor must never delete a page.\n\n➡️ **No. Only Co-VPs change a page's status.** Editors change the text only. An Editor's text change to a Published page reaches Visitors at the next Go live, with no approval step.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q12 - Is Go live a button or a terminal command?** Co-VPs change every year and your brief says each new person has a different level of computing expertise. Right now Go live means typing two commands in a terminal.\n\n➡️ **A \"Go live\" button on the dashboard, Co-VP only.** It runs the same two steps as the commands. Editors don't get it, since you said Co-VPs are responsible for step 2 and it is their laptop.\n\n---\n\nOne offer: your Q1 decision (accounts belong to positions, passwords passed down) meets the bar for an ADR. It is hard to reverse, surprising to a later reader, and you chose it over per-person accounts for a stated reason. Say yes and I'll write `ADR-003` with your reasoning.\n\nStill parked: slugs for near-identical weekly titles, what the dashboard counts, and the out-of-scope list.\n--- line 232\nRound 2 is settled. `CONTEXT.md` now also defines **Author**, **Weekly email** (your note that it matches the post plus a link), **Home**, and **Past posts**, and the Account entry says the two Co-VPs share one account.\n\nHere is round 3, which I expect to be the last full round.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q13 - What does an account sign in with?** The template's test helper signs in with an email address and a password, and the seeded demo accounts are `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. I can't see the grader's walkthrough script, but it most likely does the same. Does AWM have an email address per position, one club address, or neither?\n\n➡️ **Keep an email-shaped sign-in**, one address per account (for example a `treasurer@...` style address, real or not). The console never sends mail, so the address is only a login name. Each account also has a display name that is the position (\"Treasurer\"), and that is what shows as Author.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q14 - What is the link to a weekly post?** The link (the slug) is built from the title, and it is what goes in the Weekly email. Nearly every post is titled something like \"Study Hall tonight!\", so titles will collide. A new officer will also never have heard of a slug.\n\n➡️ **The link is made automatically from the date plus the title** (for example `2026-10-05-study-hall-tonight`), and officers never type it. **It stops changing once the post is Published**, so a link already sent in an email never breaks, even if someone fixes a typo in the title. The Monday order becomes: write the post, mark it Published, a Co-VP presses Go live, then send the email with the link.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q15 - How does a Visitor know which Monday a post means?** Your example says \"We are CURRENTLY having our study hall tonight.\" Scenario: a curious student opens Home on Thursday and reads \"tonight\".\n\n➡️ **Every post shows its date with the weekday** (\"Monday, October 5\") right above the text, on Home and on its own page. That date is when the post was first Published. Nothing expires or hides automatically.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q16 - What does the dashboard tell an officer?** Capability 5 needs counts and a list you can filter. Scenario: the Social Chair marks the reminder Published at 6pm and leaves. Nobody presses Go live, so the email link leads nowhere.\n\n➡️ **Counts of Draft posts, Published posts, and Pages**, with the number of Accounts shown to Co-VPs only. Add **\"Last Go live: date and time\"** and a plain warning when something has changed since then (\"2 changes are not live yet\"). The content list filters by Draft/Published and by Post/Page, newest first.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q17 - What is deactivating an account for?** With one account per position, nobody \"leaves\". Two scenarios: AWM has no Social Chair for a year, or AWM adds a new position such as an outreach chair.\n\n➡️ **Co-VPs deactivate an account when its position is vacant and create one when a position is added**, choosing its Role. A deactivated account cannot sign in, but **its posts stay on the site** and can be reactivated later. Accounts are never deleted.\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q18 - What is explicitly out of scope?** My proposed list of things this CMS will not do:\n\n- Send the Weekly email (it stays manual, per your brief)\n- Host the snack request form (Join & Snacks only links to the Google Form)\n- Scheduled posts or a calendar\n- Comments, or any Visitor login\n- Image uploads (this means no uploaded officer photos on About)\n- Categories, tags, or search\n- A trash bin or undo for deleted posts\n- \"Forgot my password\" emails\n- A \"copy last week's reminder\" button\n\n➡️ **All nine are out.** The last one would be useful for AWM, since 99% of posts are the same reminder, so I'd name it as the first future improvement in your report rather than build it before Tuesday. {"parentUuid":"b0cb4556-49f2-4e70-a1c0-a642bc848f9e","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjZu9g78e3Vd5MANo89p","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In session 11 (line 181, round 1 of the grill), Claude recommended **(b), one account per person**. In September, can they still log in?\n\n**Its recommendation, quoted:**\n> ➡️ **(b), per person.** Under (a) the old Social Chair still knows the password, and capability 6 (\"a deactivated user cannot log in\") has nothing to deactivate. That is the whole skeleton — it exists so you can prove the stack\nruns before you build anything on it.\n\nAdd your routes in their own modules (app/routes/posts.py and so on) and include\nthem here. Keep this file small.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, Request\nfrom fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse, Response\nfrom starlette.middleware.sessions import SessionMiddleware\n\nfrom app import accounts, content, publish, settings\nfrom app.routes import accounts as accounts_routes, auth, console, pages, posts\nfrom app.templating import templates\n\n\ndef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    accounts.init_db()\n    content.init_db()\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\")\n    # max_age=None: a session cookie with no Expires/Max-Age, so it ends when the\n    # browser closes.\n    app.add_middleware(SessionMiddleware, secret_key=settings.SECRET_KEY,\n                       session_cookie=\"session\", max_age=None,\n                       same_site=\"lax\", https_only=False)\n    app.include_router(auth.router)\n    app.include_router(console.router)\n    app.include_router(posts.router)\n    app.include_router(pages.router)\n    app.include_router(accounts_routes.router)\n\n    @app.exception_handler(accounts.NoPermission)\n    def no_permission(request: Request, exc: accounts.NoPermission):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/no_permission.html\",\n            {\"title\": \"No permission\", \"home_path\": \"/admin\"}, status_code=403)\n\n    # The local preview of the public site: the same renderers the export uses,\n    # at the same relative paths, so it shows Published content only.\n    @app.get(\"/\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_home():\n        return publish.render_front()\n\n    @app.get(\"/style.css\")\n    def public_css():\n        return Response(publish.CSS, media_type=\"text/css\")\n\n    @app.get(\"/{link}.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_page(link: str):\n        page = publish.render_page(link)\n        if page is None:\n            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"No such Page.\")\n        return page\n\n    @app.get(\"/posts/index.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_past():\n        return publish.render_past()\n\n    @app.get(\"/posts/{link}.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_post(link: str):\n        page = publish.render_post(link)\n        if page is None:\n            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"No such Post.\")\n        return page\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    #   from app.routes import posts\n    #   app.include_router(posts.router)\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":68,"oldLines":8,"newStart":68,"newLines":6,"lines":["         return page"," ","     # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.","-    #   from app.routes import posts","-    #   app.include_router(posts.router)","     return app"," "," "]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false,"contentNotInModelContext":true},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b1779fd4-af04-46f7-8a7e-52ffc5a8975a","session_id":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","version":"2.1.289","gitBranch":"main"}

That is the whole skeleton — it exists so you can prove the stack\nruns before you build anything on it.\n\nAdd your routes in their own modules (app/routes/posts.py and so on) and include\nthem here. Keep this file small.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, Request\nfrom fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse, Response\nfrom starlette.middleware.sessions import SessionMiddleware\n\nfrom app import accounts, content, publish, settings\nfrom app.routes import accounts as accounts_routes, auth, console, pages, posts\nfrom app.templating import templates\n\n\ndef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    accounts.init_db()\n    content.init_db()\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\")\n    # max_age=None: a session cookie with no Expires/Max-Age, so it ends when the\n    # browser closes.\n    app.add_middleware(SessionMiddleware, secret_key=settings.SECRET_KEY,\n                       session_cookie=\"session\", max_age=None,\n                       same_site=\"lax\", https_only=False)\n    app.include_router(auth.router)\n    app.include_router(console.router)\n    app.include_router(posts.router)\n    app.include_router(pages.router)\n    app.include_router(accounts_routes.router)\n\n    @app.exception_handler(accounts.NoPermission)\n    def no_permission(request: Request, exc: accounts.NoPermission):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/no_permission.html\",\n            {\"title\": \"No permission\", \"home_path\": \"/admin\"}, status_code=403)\n\n    # The local preview of the public site: the same renderers the export uses,\n    # at the same relative paths, so it shows Published content only.\n    @app.get(\"/\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_home():\n        return publish.render_front()\n\n    @app.get(\"/style.css\")\n    def public_css():\n        return Response(publish.CSS, media_type=\"text/css\")\n\n    @app.get(\"/{link}.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_page(link: str):\n        page = publish.render_page(link)\n        if page is None:\n            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"No such Page.\")\n        return page\n\n    @app.get(\"/posts/index.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_past():\n        return publish.render_past()\n\n    @app.get(\"/posts/{link}.html\", response_class=HTMLResponse)\n    def public_post(link: str):\n        page = publish.render_post(link)\n        if page is None:\n            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"No such Post.\")\n        return page\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":67,"oldLines":7,"newStart":67,"newLines":6,"lines":["             raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"No such Post.\")","         return page"," ","-    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.","     return app"," "," "]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false,"contentNotInModelContext":true},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7070c3f2-b197-4b7c-b2cc-2be66000e4ec","session_id":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d9d80b00-b134-4ffb-9884-96683b75be20","version":"2.1.289","gitBranch":"main"}
